Let me start by understanding the problem and exploring the repository.

## Phase 1. READING<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me carefully read and understand the issue:

1.1 Code/config snippets analysis:
The example shows a diff where:
- Original: requests==2.25.1 with two hashes and a comment "# via -r project/requirements.in"
- Updated: requests==2.22.0 with two hashes but the comment is removed

1.2 Technical details:
- Tool: hashin (Python package manager/hash updater)
- Context: pip-compile generated requirements files
- Issue: Comments starting with "#" are being stripped when updating hashes
- Method: The tool seems to parse and rewrite requirement lines

1.3 Problem explanation:
When hashin updates a package version in a requirements file that was originally generated by pip-compile, it correctly updates the package version and hashes, but it removes any trailing comments that pip-compile adds (like "# via -r project/requirements.in"). These comments are valuable metadata showing the dependency chain.

1.4 Steps to reproduce:
1. Have a requirements file with pip-compile style comments
2. Use hashin to update a package version
3. Observe that the comments after the hashes are removed

1.5 Best practices for fixing:
- Preserve existing formatting and comments
- Only modify the parts that need changing (version and hashes)
- Handle various comment formats gracefully

     1	======
     2	hashin
     3	======
     4	
     5	.. image:: https://github.com/peterbe/hashin/workflows/Python/badge.svg
     6	    :target: https://github.com/peterbe/hashin/actions
     7	
     8	.. image:: https://badge.fury.io/py/hashin.svg
     9	    :target: https://pypi.python.org/pypi/hashin
    10	
    11	Helps you write your ``requirements.txt`` with hashes so you can
    12	install with ``pip install --require-hashes -r ...``
    13	
    14	If you want to add a package or edit the version of one you're currently
    15	using you have to do the following steps:
    16	
    17	1. Go to pypi for that package
    18	2. Download the ``.tgz`` file
    19	3. Possibly download the ``.whl`` file
    20	4. Run ``pip hash downloadedpackage-1.2.3.tgz``
    21	5. Run ``pip hash downloadedpackage-1.2.3.whl``
    22	6. Edit ``requirements.txt``
    23	
    24	This script does all those things.
    25	Hackishly wonderfully so.
    26	
    27	A Word of Warning!
    28	==================
    29	
    30	The whole point of hashing is that you **vet the packages** that you use
    31	on your laptop and that they haven't been tampered with. Then you
    32	can confidently install them on a server.
    33	
    34	This tool downloads from PyPI (over HTTPS) and runs ``pip hash``
    35	on the downloaded files.
    36	
    37	You should check that the packages that are downloaded
    38	are sane and not tampered with. The way you do that is to run
    39	``hashin`` as normal but with the ``--verbose`` flag. When you do that
    40	it will print where it downloaded the relevant files and those
    41	files are not deleted. For example::
    42	
    43	    $ hashin --verbose bgg /tmp/reqs.txt
    44	    https://pypi.python.org/pypi/bgg/json
    45	    * Latest version for 0.22.1
    46	    * Found URL https://pypi.python.org/packages/2.7/b/bgg/bgg-0.22.1-py2-none-any.whl
    47	    *   Re-using /var/folders/1x/2hf5hbs902q54g3bgby5bzt40000gn/T/bgg-0.22.1-py2-none-any.whl
    48	    *   Hash e5172c3fda0e8a42d1797fd1ff75245c3953d7c8574089a41a219204dbaad83d
    49	    * Found URL https://pypi.python.org/packages/source/b/bgg/bgg-0.22.1.tar.gz
    50	    *   Re-using /var/folders/1x/2hf5hbs902q54g3bgby5bzt40000gn/T/bgg-0.22.1.tar.gz
    51	    *   Hash aaa53aea1cecb8a6e1288d6bfe52a51408a264a97d5c865c38b34ae16c9bff88
    52	    * Editing /tmp/reqs.txt
    53	
    54	You might not have time to go through the lines one by one
    55	but you should be aware that the vetting process is your
    56	responsibility.
    57	
    58	Installation
    59	============
    60	
    61	This is something you only do or ever need in a development
    62	environment. Ie. your laptop::
    63	
    64	    pip install hashin
    65	
    66	How to use it
    67	=============
    68	
    69	Suppose you want to install ``futures``. You can either do this::
    70	
    71	    hashin futures
    72	
    73	Which will download the latest version tarball (and wheel) and
    74	calculate their pip hash and edit your ``requirements.txt`` file.
    75	
    76	Or you can be specific about exactly which version you want::
    77	
    78	    hashin "futures==2.1.3"
    79	
    80	You can also specify more than one package at a time::
    81	
    82	    hashin "futures==2.1.3" requests
    83	
    84	Suppose you don't have a ``requirements.txt`` right there in the same
    85	directory you can specify ``--requirements-file``::
    86	
    87	    hashin futures --requirements-file=stuff/requirements/prod.txt
    88	
    89	By default ``sha256`` hashes are used, but this can be overridden using the
    90	``--algorithm`` argument::
    91	
    92	    hashin futures --algorithm=sha512
    93	
    94	If there's no output, it worked. Check how it edited your
    95	requirements file.
    96	
    97	Filtering releases by Python version
    98	====================================
    99	
   100	Some requirements have many releases built for different versions of Python and
   101	different architectures. These hashes aren't useful in some cases, if those
   102	wheels don't work with your project. ``hashin`` can filter on the Python
   103	version to skip these extraneous hashes.
   104	
   105	For example, the ``cffi`` package offers wheels built for many versions of
   106	CPython from 2.6 to 3.5. To select only one of them, you can use the
   107	``--python-version`` option::
   108	
   109	    hashin "cffi==1.5.2" --python-version 3.5
   110	
   111	If you need to support multiple versions, you can pass this option multiple
   112	times::
   113	
   114	    hashin "cffi==1.5.2" --python-version 2.7 --python-version 3.5
   115	
   116	``hashin`` will expand these Python versions to a full list of identifers that
   117	could be found on PyPI. For example, ``3.5`` will expand to match any of
   118	``3.5``, ``py3``, ``py3.5``, ``py2.py3``, or ``cp3.5``. You can also specify
   119	these exact identifiers directly, if you need something specific.
   120	
   121	The ``source`` release is always automatically included. ``pip`` will use
   122	this as a fallback in the case a suitable wheel cannot be found.
   123	
   124	Dry run mode
   125	============
   126	
   127	There are some use cases, when you maybe don't want to edit your ``requirements.txt``
   128	right away. You can use the ``--dry-run`` argument to show the diff, so you
   129	can preview the changes to your ``requirements.txt`` file.
   130	
   131	Example::
   132	
   133	    hashin --dry-run requests==2.19.1
   134	
   135	Would result in a printout on the command line::
   136	
   137	    --- Old
   138	    +++ New
   139	    @@ -0,0 +1,3 @@
   140	    +requests==2.19.1 \
   141	    +    --hash=sha256:63b52e3c866428a224f97cab011de738c36aec0185aa91cfacd418b5d58911d1 \
   142	    +    --hash=sha256:ec22d826a36ed72a7358ff3fe56cbd4ba69dd7a6718ffd450ff0e9df7a47ce6a
   143	
   144	PEP-0496 Environment Markers
   145	============================
   146	
   147	Requirements can use `PEP-0496`_ style specifiers (e.g. like
   148	``cffi==1.5.2; python_version >= '3.4'``) and these will be passed
   149	through when re-writing the ``requirements.txt`` file. ``hashin`` doesn't
   150	parse the specifiers themselves and will take anything after the
   151	semicolon. If you are using ``python_version`` you will still need to
   152	pass appropriate options if you don't want every available hash.
   153	
   154	An example of this might be::
   155	
   156	    hashin "pywin32-ctypes ; sys_platform == 'win32'"
   157	
   158	which will result it something like this in the ``requirements.txt`` file::
   159	
   160	    pywin32-ctypes==0.1.2; sys_platform == 'win32' \
   161	        --hash=sha256:4820b830f42e6889d34142bcd07b3896018c3620d8c31f5e13b72caf1f4d1d0f
   162	
   163	And if you want to limit it to certain Python versions, here's an example::
   164	
   165	    hashin "cffi==1.5.2; python_version >= '3.4'" -p 3.4 -p 3.5
   166	
   167	
   168	.. _`PEP-0496`: https://www.python.org/dev/peps/pep-0496/
   169	
   170	Using as a Python library
   171	=========================
   172	
   173	Everything you can do with ``hashin`` on the command line you can do
   174	in running Python too. For example::
   175	
   176	    import hashin
   177	    from pprint import pprint
   178	    pprint(hashin.get_package_hashes('Django'))
   179	
   180	This will print out::
   181	
   182	    {'hashes': [{'hash': 'fbc7ffaa45a4a67cb45f77dbd94e8eceecebe1d0959fe9c665dfbf28b41899e6',
   183	             'url': 'https://pypi.python.org/packages/41/c1/68dd27946b03a3d756b0ff665baad25aee1f59918891d86ab76764209208/Django-1.11b1-py2.py3-none-any.whl'}],
   184	    'package': 'Django',
   185	    'version': '1.11b1'}
   186	
   187	Or with specific version, algorithm and certain Python versions::
   188	
   189	    import hashin
   190	    from pprint import pprint
   191	    pprint(hashin.get_package_hashes(
   192	        'Django',
   193	        version='1.10',
   194	        algorithm='sha512',
   195	        python_versions=('3.5',)
   196	    ))
   197	
   198	Local development
   199	=================
   200	
   201	After you have cloned the project, created a virtual environment and run:
   202	
   203	    pip install -e ".[dev]"
   204	
   205	Now, to run it you can use the installed executable ``hashin`` and do things
   206	like::
   207	
   208	    touch /tmp/reqs.txt
   209	    hashin -r /tmp/reqs.txt Django
   210	
   211	
   212	Running tests
   213	=============
   214	
   215	Simply run::
   216	
   217	    python setup.py test
   218	
   219	When you use ``pip install ".[dev]"`` it will install ``tox`` which you can use
   220	to run the full test suites (plus linting) in different Python environments::
   221	
   222	    tox
   223	
   224	Running tests with test coverage
   225	================================
   226	
   227	To run the tests with test coverage, with ``pytest`` run something like
   228	this::
   229	
   230	    $ pip install pytest-cover
   231	    $ pytest --cov=hashin --cov-report=html
   232	    $ open htmlcov/index.html
   233	
   234	
   235	Debugging
   236	=========
   237	
   238	To avoid having to install ``hashin`` just to test it or debug a feature
   239	you can simply just run it like this::
   240	
   241	    touch /tmp/whatever.txt
   242	    python hashin.py --verbose Django /tmp/whatever.txt
   243	
   244	
   245	Code Style
   246	==========
   247	
   248	All Python code should be run through `Black <https://pypi.org/project/black/>`_.
   249	This is checked in CI and you can test it locally with ``tox``.
   250	
   251	Also, this project uses `therapist <https://pypi.org/project/therapist/>`_
   252	which helps with checking code style as a git pre-commit hook. ``therapist``
   253	is used in ``tox``. To run all code style checks, use ``tox -e lint`` but
   254	make sure your version of ``tox`` is built on a Python 3.
   255	
   256	History
   257	=======
   258	
   259	This program is a "fork" of https://pypi.python.org/pypi/peepin
   260	``peepin`` was a companion to the program ``peep``
   261	https://pypi.python.org/pypi/peep/ but the functionality of ``peep``
   262	has been put directly into ``pip`` as of version 8.
   263	
   264	Future
   265	======
   266	
   267	If this script proves itself to work and be useful, I hope we can
   268	put it directly into ``pip``.
   269

        ********************************************************************************
        Requirements should be satisfied by a PEP 517 installer.
        If you are using pip, you can try `pip install --use-pep517`.
        ********************************************************************************

   384	def amend_requirements_content(requirements, all_new_lines):
   385	    # I wish we had types!
   386	    assert isinstance(all_new_lines, list), type(all_new_lines)
   387	
   388	    padding = " " * 4
   389	
   390	    def is_different_lines(old_lines, new_lines, indent):
   391	        # This regex is used to only temporarily normalize the names of packages
   392	        # in the lines being compared. This results in "old" names matching
   393	        # "new" names so that hashin correctly replaces them when it looks for
   394	        # them.
   395	        match_delims = re.compile(r"[-_]")
   396	
   397	        # This assumes that the package is already mentioned in the old
   398	        # requirements. Now we just need to double-check that its lines are
   399	        # different.
   400	        # The 'new_lines` is what we might intend to replace it with.
   401	        old = set([match_delims.sub("-", line.strip(" \\")) for line in old_lines])
   402	        new = set([indent + x.strip(" \\") for x in new_lines])
   403	        return old != new
   404	
   405	    for package, old_name, new_text in all_new_lines:
   406	        # The call to `escape` will turn hyphens into escaped hyphens
   407	        #
   408	        # ex.
   409	        #   -       becomes     \\-
   410	        #
   411	        escaped = re.escape(old_name)
   412	
   413	        # This changes those escaped hypens into a pattern to match
   414	        #
   415	        # ex.
   416	        #   \\-     becomes     [-_]
   417	        #
   418	        # This is necessary so that hashin will correctly find underscored (old)
   419	        # and hyphenated (new) package names so that it will correctly replace an
   420	        # old name with the new name when there is a version update.
   421	        escape_replaced = escaped.replace("\\-", "[-_]")
   422	        regex = re.compile(
   423	            r"^(?P<indent>[ \t]*){0}(\[.*\])?==".format(escape_replaced),
   424	            re.IGNORECASE | re.MULTILINE,
   425	        )
   426	        # if the package wasn't already there, add it to the bottom
   427	        match = regex.search(requirements)
   428	        if not match:
   429	            # easy peasy
   430	            if requirements:
   431	                requirements = requirements.strip() + "\n"
   432	            requirements += new_text.strip() + "\n"
   433	        else:
   434	            indent = match.group("indent")
   435	            lines = []
   436	            for line in requirements.splitlines():
   437	                if regex.search(line):
   438	                    lines.append(line)
   439	                elif lines and line.startswith(indent + padding):
   440	                    lines.append(line)
   441	                elif lines:
   442	                    break
   443	            if is_different_lines(lines, new_text.splitlines(), indent):
   444	                # need to replace the existing
   445	                combined = "\n".join(lines + [""])
   446	                # indent non-empty lines
   447	                indented = re.sub(
   448	                    r"^(.+)$", r"{0}\1".format(indent), new_text, flags=re.MULTILINE
   449	                )
   450	                requirements = requirements.replace(combined, indented)
   451	
   452	    return requirements
   453	
   454	
   455	def get_latest_version(data, include_prereleases):
   456	    """
   457	    Return the version string of what we think is the latest version.
   458	    In the data blob from PyPI there is the info->version key which
   459	    is just the latest in time. Ideally we want the latest non-pre-release.
   460	    """
   461	    if not data.get("releases"):
   462	        # If there were no releases, fall back to the old way of doing
   463	        # things with the info->version key.
   464	        # This feels kinda strange but it has worked for years
   465	        return data["info"]["version"]
   466	    all_versions = []
   467	    count_prereleases = 0
   468	    for version in data["releases"]:
   469	        v = parse(version)
   470	        if not v.is_prerelease or include_prereleases:

"""Test script to reproduce the issue where hashin removes comments when updating packages."""

def test_comment_preservation_issue():
    """Reproduce the issue where comments after hashes are removed."""

    --hash=sha256:c210084e36a42ae6b9219e00e48287def368a26d03a048ddad7bfee44f75871e

    --hash=sha256:9cf5292fcd0f598c671cfc1e0d7d1a7f13bb8085e9a590f48c010551dc6c4b31"""
    )

    print("=== ORIGINAL REQUIREMENTS ===")

    print(requirements)

    print("\n=== NEW LINES TO INSERT (formatted) ===")
    print(new_lines[2])

    # Apply the amendment

    print(repr(result))
    print("\n=== RESULT AFTER AMENDMENT (formatted) ===")
    print(result)
    
    # Check if the comment is preserved
    if "# via -r project/requirements.in" in result:
        print("\n✅ SUCCESS: Comment was preserved!")
    else:
        print("\n❌ FAILURE: Comment was removed!")
        
    return result

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me analyze the problem:

5.1 What the problem is:
When hashin updates a package in a requirements file, it correctly identifies the package lines and replaces them. However, it only collects lines that either:
- Match the initial package line pattern, or  
- Start with the same indent plus padding (continuation lines with hashes)

Comments that come after the hash lines are not collected because they don't match the continuation pattern, so they get lost during replacement.

5.2 Where the problem is located:
In the `amend_requirements_content` function in hashin.py, specifically lines 436-442 where lines are collected for replacement.

5.3 How the test reproduces the issue:
My test creates a requirements file with a package entry that has hash lines followed by a comment line. When hashin tries to replace this package, it only collects the package line and hash lines, but not the comment line, causing the comment to be lost.

5.4 Best practices to take into account:
- Preserve existing formatting and comments
- Only modify what needs to be changed (version and hashes)  
- Don't break existing functionality
- Handle edge cases gracefully
- Keep the fix minimal and focused

5.5 How to fix the problem:
The issue is in the line collection logic. Currently it stops collecting lines as soon as it finds a line that doesn't match the continuation pattern. Instead, it should:

1. Continue collecting lines that are comments (start with #) that have the same or greater indentation as the package line
2. Stop only when it encounters a line that's clearly a new package or end of file
3. Be careful not to collect unrelated comments

Actually, looking more carefully at the logic:
- Line 437: if regex.search(line): lines.append(line) - collects the initial package line
- Line 439: elif lines and line.startswith(indent + padding): lines.append(line) - collects continuation lines (hashes)
- Line 441: elif lines: break - stops collecting once we have some lines and hit a non-matching line

The fix should allow collecting comment lines that come after hash lines but before the next package. We need to modify the condition to also collect comment lines with appropriate indentation.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me examine the logic more carefully and also check how pip-compile typically formats these comments:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/peterbe__hashin__unknown/test_reproduce_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python

    result = amend_requirements_content(requirements, [new_lines])
    
    print("\n=== RESULT AFTER AMENDMENT ===")
    print(repr(result))
    print("\n=== RESULT AFTER AMENDMENT (formatted) ===")
    print(result)
    
    # Check if the comment is preserved
    if "# via -r project/requirements.in" in result:
        print("\n✅ SUCCESS: Comment was preserved!")
    else:
        print("\n❌ FAILURE: Comment was removed!")
        
    return result

## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION

Looking at the logic in `amend_requirements_content`, the issue is in lines 436-442. The current logic:

1. Find the package line 
2. Collect continuation lines (those starting with indent + padding)
3. Stop when encountering any line that doesn't match either pattern

I need to modify this to also collect comment lines that come after hash lines. """Debug script to understand the line collection logic."""

import re
from hashin import amend_requirements_content


def debug_line_collection():
    """Debug the line collection process."""
    
    # Simulate a requirements file with pip-compile style comments
    requirements = """requests==2.25.1 \\
    --hash=sha256:27973dd4a904a4f13b263a19c866c13b92a39ed1c964655f025f3f8d3d75b804 \\
    --hash=sha256:c210084e36a42ae6b9219e00e48287def368a26d03a048ddad7bfee44f75871e
    # via -r project/requirements.in
other-package==1.0.0 \\
    --hash=sha256:somehashhere
"""

    print("=== DEBUGGING LINE COLLECTION ===")
    print("Requirements:")
    print(repr(requirements))
    
    # Simulate the regex matching logic
    old_name = "requests"
    escaped = re.escape(old_name)
    escape_replaced = escaped.replace("\\-", "[-_]")
    regex = re.compile(
        r"^(?P<indent>[ \t]*){0}(\[.*\])?==".format(escape_replaced),
        re.IGNORECASE | re.MULTILINE,
    )
    
    print(f"\nRegex pattern: {regex.pattern}")
    
    match = regex.search(requirements)
    if match:
        print(f"Match found! Indent: '{match.group('indent')}'")
        indent = match.group("indent")
        padding = " " * 4
        
        print(f"Expected continuation prefix: '{indent + padding}'")
        
        lines = []
        print("\nLine-by-line analysis:")
        for i, line in enumerate(requirements.splitlines()):
            print(f"Line {i}: {repr(line)}")
            
            if regex.search(line):
                print(f"  -> Matches package line, adding to lines")
                lines.append(line)
            elif lines and line.startswith(indent + padding):
                print(f"  -> Continuation line, adding to lines")
                lines.append(line)
            elif lines and line.lstrip().startswith('#') and len(line) > len(line.lstrip()):
                print(f"  -> Comment line detected")
                line_indent = line[:len(line) - len(line.lstrip())]
                print(f"     Line indent: '{line_indent}', Package indent: '{indent}'")
                if line_indent.startswith(indent):
                    print(f"  -> Comment with proper indent, adding to lines")
                    lines.append(line)
                else:
                    print(f"  -> Comment indent doesn't match, stopping")
                    break
            elif lines:
                print(f"  -> Non-matching line after package lines, stopping")
                break
            else:
                print(f"  -> Not part of our package, skipping")
        
        print(f"\nCollected lines ({len(lines)}):")
        for i, line in enumerate(lines):
            print(f"  {i}: {repr(line)}")
    else:
        print("No match found!")


if __name__ == "__main__":

   471	                    
   472	                    # Add the comment lines
   473	                    for comment_line in comment_lines_to_preserve:
   474	                        extended_new_text += comment_line + '\n'
   475	                    
   476	                    # indent non-empty lines
   477	                    indented = re.sub(
   478	                        r"^(.+)$", r"{0}\1".format(indent), extended_new_text.rstrip(), flags=re.MULTILINE
   479	                    )
   480	                else:
   481	                    # indent non-empty lines
   482	                    indented = re.sub(
   483	                        r"^(.+)$", r"{0}\1".format(indent), new_text, flags=re.MULTILINE
   484	                    )
   485	                requirements = requirements.replace(combined, indented)
   486	
   487	    return requirements
   488	
   489	
   490

"""Comprehensive test for comment preservation functionality."""

from hashin import amend_requirements_content


def test_basic_comment_preservation():
    """Test basic comment preservation with pip-compile style comments."""
    requirements = """requests==2.25.1 \\
    --hash=sha256:27973dd4a904a4f13b263a19c866c13b92a39ed1c964655f025f3f8d3d75b804 \\
    --hash=sha256:c210084e36a42ae6b9219e00e48287def368a26d03a048ddad7bfee44f75871e
    # via -r project/requirements.in
other-package==1.0.0 \\
    --hash=sha256:somehashhere
"""
    
    new_lines = (
        "requests",
        "requests", 
        """requests==2.22.0 \\
    --hash=sha256:11e007a8a2aa0323f5a921e9e6a2d7e4e67d9877e85773fba9ba6419025cbeb4 \\
    --hash=sha256:9cf5292fcd0f598c671cfc1e0d7d1a7f13bb8085e9a590f48c010551dc6c4b31
"""
    )
    
    result = amend_requirements_content(requirements, [new_lines])
    assert "# via -r project/requirements.in" in result
    print("✅ Basic comment preservation test passed")

    )

    assert "# another comment" in result
    assert "# yet another comment" in result

def test_comment_not_preserved_when_not_related():
    """Test that unrelated comments are not mistakenly preserved."""
    requirements = """# This is a general comment
requests==2.25.1 \\
    --hash=sha256:27973dd4a904a4f13b263a19c866c13b92a39ed1c964655f025f3f8d3d75b804 \\
    --hash=sha256:c210084e36a42ae6b9219e00e48287def368a26d03a048ddad7bfee44f75871e

# This comment should not be preserved
other-package==1.0.0 \\
    --hash=sha256:somehashhere
"""
    
    new_lines = (
        "requests",
        "requests", 
        """requests==2.22.0 \\
    --hash=sha256:11e007a8a2aa0323f5a921e9e6a2d7e4e67d9877e85773fba9ba6419025cbeb4 \\
    --hash=sha256:9cf5292fcd0f598c671cfc1e0d7d1a7f13bb8085e9a590f48c010551dc6c4b31
"""
    )
    
    result = amend_requirements_content(requirements, [new_lines])
    # The unrelated comment "# This comment should not be preserved" should not be in result
    # But the package-level comment (if any) should be preserved
    print("✅ Unrelated comment handling test passed")

            assert "readme-renderer==26.0" in output

def test_amend_requirements_content_preserves_pip_compile_comments():
    """Test that pip-compile style comments are preserved when updating packages."""
    requirements = """requests==2.25.1 \\
    --hash=sha256:27973dd4a904a4f13b263a19c866c13b92a39ed1c964655f025f3f8d3d75b804 \\
    --hash=sha256:c210084e36a42ae6b9219e00e48287def368a26d03a048ddad7bfee44f75871e
    # via -r project/requirements.in
other-package==1.0.0 \\
    --hash=sha256:somehashhere
"""

    new_lines = (
        "requests",
        "requests", 
        """requests==2.22.0 \\
    --hash=sha256:11e007a8a2aa0323f5a921e9e6a2d7e4e67d9877e85773fba9ba6419025cbeb4 \\
    --hash=sha256:9cf5292fcd0f598c671cfc1e0d7d1a7f13bb8085e9a590f48c010551dc6c4b31
"""
    )

    result = hashin.amend_requirements_content(requirements, [new_lines])
    
    # The comment should be preserved
    assert "# via -r project/requirements.in" in result
    
    # The updated package should be present
    assert "requests==2.22.0" in result
    assert "sha256:11e007a8a2aa0323f5a921e9e6a2d7e4e67d9877e85773fba9ba6419025cbeb4" in result
    assert "sha256:9cf5292fcd0f598c671cfc1e0d7d1a7f13bb8085e9a590f48c010551dc6c4b31" in result
    
    # The other package should remain unchanged
    assert "other-package==1.0.0" in result

"""Final verification that the exact issue from the problem description is fixed."""

from hashin import amend_requirements_content


def main():
    print("🔍 Verifying the exact issue from the problem description...")
    print()
    
    # BEFORE state (from the issue)
    before_state = """requests==2.25.1 \\
    --hash=sha256:27973dd4a904a4f13b263a19c866c13b92a39ed1c964655f025f3f8d3d75b804 \\
    --hash=sha256:c210084e36a42ae6b9219e00e48287def368a26d03a048ddad7bfee44f75871e
    # via -r project/requirements.in"""

        "requests",
        "requests",
        """requests==2.22.0 \\
    --hash=sha256:11e007a8a2aa0323f5a921e9e6a2d7e4e67d9877e85773fba9ba6419025cbeb4 \\
    --hash=sha256:9cf5292fcd0f598c671cfc1e0d7d1a7f13bb8085e9a590f48c010551dc6c4b31"""
    )

    print("BEFORE (what pip-compile generated):")
    print(before_state)
    print()
    
    # Apply hashin update
    after_state = amend_requirements_content(before_state, [new_content])
    
    print("AFTER (what hashin produces with our fix):")
    print(after_state)
    print()
    
    # Verification
    if "# via -r project/requirements.in" in after_state:
        print("✅ SUCCESS: The pip-compile comment is preserved!")
        print("✅ The issue has been RESOLVED!")
        return True
    else:
        print("❌ FAILURE: The pip-compile comment was lost!")
        print("❌ The issue still exists!")

   484	                    )
   485	                requirements = requirements.replace(combined, indented)
   486	
   487	    return requirements

"""Final verification that the exact issue from the problem description is fixed."""

from hashin import amend_requirements_content


def main():
    print("🔍 Verifying the exact issue from the problem description...")
    print()
    
    # BEFORE state (from the issue)
    before_state = """requests==2.25.1 \\
    --hash=sha256:27973dd4a904a4f13b263a19c866c13b92a39ed1c964655f025f3f8d3d75b804 \\
    --hash=sha256:c210084e36a42ae6b9219e00e48287def368a26d03a048ddad7bfee44f75871e
    # via -r project/requirements.in"""

        "requests",
        "requests",

    --hash=sha256:11e007a8a2aa0323f5a921e9e6a2d7e4e67d9877e85773fba9ba6419025cbeb4 \\
    --hash=sha256:9cf5292fcd0f598c671cfc1e0d7d1a7f13bb8085e9a590f48c010551dc6c4b31"""
    )

    print("BEFORE (what pip-compile generated):")
    print(before_state)
    print()
    
    # Apply hashin update
    after_state = amend_requirements_content(before_state, [new_content])

    print(after_state)
    print()
    
    # Verification
    if "# via -r project/requirements.in" in after_state and "requests==2.22.0" in after_state:
        print("✅ SUCCESS: The pip-compile comment is preserved AND version updated!")
        print("✅ The issue has been RESOLVED!")

        return True

        print("❌ The issue still exists!")
        return False
